#define RRAY_REDUCE_ARGS(...) , __VA_ARGS__
#define RRAY_REDUCE_NO_ARGS

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// rray_reduce

// Flat reduction is useful when the output element is the only state carried
// between input elements. It walks `x` once in storage order, updating the
// output element that each input contributes to. This is faster than nested
// reduction because it reads `x` linearly and avoids a separate inner traversal
// for each output element.

typedef r_obj* (*rray_reduce_fn)(
  r_obj* x,
  r_ssize out_size,
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan,
  struct r_lazy error_call
);

typedef rray_reduce_fn (*rray_reduce_fn_switch)(
  r_obj* x,
  bool na_rm,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
);

r_obj* rray_reduce(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* axes,
  bool na_rm,
  rray_reduce_fn_switch fn_switch,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
);

#define RRAY_REDUCE(                                                           \
  X_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  X_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  OUT_RTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_CTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_DEREF,                                                                   \
  OUT_INIT,                                                                    \
  ONE,                                                                         \
  ONE_ARGS                                                                     \
)                                                                              \
  const r_ssize size = rray_strided_iterator_plan_size(plan);                  \
                                                                               \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(OUT_RTYPE, out_size));                      \
  OUT_CTYPE* v_out = OUT_DEREF(out);                                           \
                                                                               \
  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {                                     \
    v_out[i] = OUT_INIT;                                                       \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);                                       \
                                                                               \
  r_ssize run_start = 0;                                                       \
  const r_ssize run_size = rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(plan);          \
                                                                               \
  r_ssize out_start = 0;                                                       \
  const r_ssize out_run_stride = rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_stride(plan);  \
                                                                               \
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];                                    \
  rray_strided_iterator_plan_point_init(plan, v_point);                        \
                                                                               \
  while (run_start != size) {                                                  \
    const r_ssize run_end = run_start + run_size;                              \
    r_ssize out_loc = out_start;                                               \
                                                                               \
    if (out_run_stride == 0) {                                                 \
      for (r_ssize i = run_start; i < run_end; ++i) {                          \
        v_out[out_loc] = ONE(v_out[out_loc], v_x[i] ONE_ARGS);                 \
      }                                                                        \
    } else {                                                                   \
      for (r_ssize i = run_start; i < run_end; ++i) {                          \
        v_out[out_loc] = ONE(v_out[out_loc], v_x[i] ONE_ARGS);                 \
        out_loc += out_run_stride;                                             \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
                                                                               \
    run_start = run_end;                                                       \
    RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_NEXT(out_start, v_point, plan);                      \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  FREE(1);                                                                     \
  return out;

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// rray_reduce_nested

// Nested reduction is needed when an output element cannot hold all reduction
// state, such as the sum and count required by a mean. The outer plan walks the
// retained axes to select each output element, and the inner plan walks the
// reduction axes to compute it. Conceptually, this is like using
// `rray_permute_axes()` to bring the reduction axes to the front, without
// materializing the permuted array.

typedef r_obj* (*rray_reduce_nested_fn)(
  r_obj* x,
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* outer_plan,
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* inner_plan,
  struct r_lazy error_call
);

typedef rray_reduce_nested_fn (*rray_reduce_nested_fn_switch)(
  r_obj* x,
  bool na_rm,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
);

r_obj* rray_reduce_nested(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* axes,
  bool na_rm,
  rray_reduce_nested_fn_switch fn_switch,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
);

#define RRAY_REDUCE_OUTER(                                                     \
  X_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  X_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  OUT_RTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_CTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_DEREF,                                                                   \
  ONE,                                                                         \
  ONE_ARGS                                                                     \
)                                                                              \
  const r_ssize size = rray_strided_iterator_plan_size(outer_plan);            \
                                                                               \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(OUT_RTYPE, size));                          \
  OUT_CTYPE* v_out = OUT_DEREF(out);                                           \
                                                                               \
  const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);                                       \
                                                                               \
  r_ssize out_run_start = 0;                                                   \
  const r_ssize out_run_size =                                                 \
    rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_size(outer_plan);                           \
                                                                               \
  r_ssize x_start = 0;                                                         \

    rray_strided_iterator_plan_run_stride(outer_plan);                         \
                                                                               \
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];                                    \
  rray_strided_iterator_plan_point_init(outer_plan, v_point);                  \
                                                                               \
  while (out_run_start != size) {                                              \

Thinking

Both drivers are shared across operations and their kernel signatures still reference the old plan. I'll first read the add and sum worked examples in the plan to confirm the intended loop shapes before making any changes.

Bash: sed -n 187,295p plans/iterators.md

## Add

Two inputs, walking the output. Each input is either fixed or moving along the run, so there are four cases. The integer version passes `error_call` to its one-element kernel directly, with no `RRAY_BINARY_ARGS()` needed.

```c
static r_obj* rray_add_int_int(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  const struct rray_strided_iterator2_plan* plan,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const r_ssize size = rray_strided_iterator2_plan_size(plan);

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_integer(size));
  int* v_out = r_int_begin(out);

  const int* v_x = r_int_cbegin(x);
  const int* v_y = r_int_cbegin(y);

  struct rray_run_iterator2 it = rray_run_iterator2(plan);

  while (rray_run_iterator2_next(&it)) {
    r_ssize x_loc = it.loc1;
    r_ssize y_loc = it.loc2;

    if (it.stride1 == 0 && it.stride2 == 0) {
      const int elt = rray_add_int_one(v_x[x_loc], v_y[y_loc], error_call);
      for (r_ssize i = it.start; i < it.end; ++i) {
        v_out[i] = elt;
      }
    } else if (it.stride1 == 0) {
      const int x_elt = v_x[x_loc];
      for (r_ssize i = it.start; i < it.end; ++i) {
        v_out[i] = rray_add_int_one(x_elt, v_y[y_loc], error_call);
        y_loc += it.stride2;
      }
    } else if (it.stride2 == 0) {
      const int y_elt = v_y[y_loc];
      for (r_ssize i = it.start; i < it.end; ++i) {
        v_out[i] = rray_add_int_one(v_x[x_loc], y_elt, error_call);
        x_loc += it.stride1;
      }
    } else {
      for (r_ssize i = it.start; i < it.end; ++i) {
        v_out[i] = rray_add_int_one(v_x[x_loc], v_y[y_loc], error_call);
        x_loc += it.stride1;
        y_loc += it.stride2;
      }
    }
  }

  FREE(1);
  return out;
}
```

The double version is the same with `double`, `r_alloc_double()`, `r_dbl_begin()` / `r_dbl_cbegin()` and `rray_add_dbl_one(x, y)`.

For example, `[4000, 4000] + [1, 4000]` gives 4000 runs where `x` moves (stride 1) and `y` is fixed (stride 0), so every run takes the third branch. `[4000, 4000] + [4000, 4000]` coalesces into one run of 16 million elements where both move.

## Sum

One input, walking the input and adding into the output. When the output's stride is 0, the whole run feeds one output, and the total is kept in a local variable.

```c
static r_obj* rray_sum_dbl(
  r_obj* x,
  r_ssize out_size,
  const struct rray_strided_iterator_plan* plan,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_double(out_size));
  double* v_out = r_dbl_begin(out);

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {
    v_out[i] = 0.0;
  }

  const double* v_x = r_dbl_cbegin(x);

  struct rray_run_iterator it = rray_run_iterator(plan);

  while (rray_run_iterator_next(&it)) {
    if (it.stride == 0) {
      double sum = v_out[it.loc];

      for (r_ssize i = it.start; i < it.end; ++i) {
        sum = rray_sum_dbl_one(sum, v_x[i]);
      }

      v_out[it.loc] = sum;
    } else {
      r_ssize out_loc = it.loc;

      for (r_ssize i = it.start; i < it.end; ++i) {
        v_out[out_loc] = rray_sum_dbl_one(v_out[out_loc], v_x[i]);
        out_loc += it.stride;
      }
    }
  }

  FREE(1);
  return out;
}
```

For example, summing `[4000, 4000]` over axis 1 gives 4000 runs with output stride 0: each run is one column, added into one output. Summing over axis 2 gives 4000 runs with output stride 1: each run adds one column into all 4000 outputs at once, which the compiler vectorizes.

## What this means for `plans/accuracy.md`
Thinking

I'll keep the loop bodies in RRAY_BINARY() and RRAY_REDUCE() unchanged, only swapping the iterator, so the benchmark isolates that change. Since the shared drivers rray_binary_arithmetic() and rray_reduce() still use the old plan for other ops, I'm adding new _run() variants just for add and sum, then will migrate the rest and delete the old drivers later.

+    y_dimensionality,
+    dimensionality,
+    v_y_broadcast_strides
+  );
+
+  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY * 2];
+  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
+    v_strides[axis * 2] = v_x_broadcast_strides[axis];
+    v_strides[axis * 2 + 1] = v_y_broadcast_strides[axis];
+  }
+
+  r_obj* out =
+    KEEP(fn(x, y, v_dimensions, dimensionality, v_strides, error_call));
+  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, dimensions);
+
+  r_obj* out_names = KEEP(rray_broadcast_names2(x, y, dimensions));
+
+  if (out_names != r_null) {
+    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, out_names);
+  }
+
+  FREE(5);
+  return out;
+}

-static r_obj* rray_sum_lgl(

+#define RRAY_SUM(                                                              \
+  X_CTYPE,                                                                     \
+  X_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
+  OUT_RTYPE,                                                                   \
+  OUT_CTYPE,                                                                   \
+  OUT_DEREF,                                                                   \
+  OUT_INIT,                                                                    \
+  ONE,                                                                         \
+  ONE_ARGS                                                                     \
+)                                                                              \
+  struct rray_run_iterator it = rray_run_iterator(                             \
+    v_x_dimensions,                                                            \
+    dimensionality,                                                            \
+    v_out_broadcast_strides,                                                   \
+    1                                                                          \
+  );                                                                           \
+                                                                               \
+  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(OUT_RTYPE, out_size));                      \
+  OUT_CTYPE* v_out = OUT_DEREF(out);                                           \
+                                                                               \
+  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {                                     \
+    v_out[i] = OUT_INIT;                                                       \
+  }                                                                            \
+                                                                               \
+  const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);                                       \
+                                                                               \
+  for (; !rray_run_iterator_done(&it); rray_run_iterator_next(&it, 1)) {       \
+    const r_ssize start = rray_run_iterator_start(&it);                        \
+    const r_ssize end = rray_run_iterator_end(&it);                            \
+                                                                               \
+    r_ssize out_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(&it, 0);                           \
+    const r_ssize out_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(&it, 0);               \
+                                                                               \
+    if (out_stride == 0) {                                                     \
+      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
+        v_out[out_loc] = ONE(v_out[out_loc], v_x[i] ONE_ARGS);                 \
+      }                                                                        \
+    } else {                                                                   \
+      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
+        v_out[out_loc] = ONE(v_out[out_loc], v_x[i] ONE_ARGS);                 \
+        out_loc += out_stride;                                                 \
+      }                                                                        \
+    }                                                                          \
+  }                                                                            \
+                                                                               \
+  FREE(1);                                                                     \
+  return out;
+
+static r_obj* rray_sum_lgl(

args <- commandArgs(trailingOnly = TRUE)
lib <- args[[1]]
label <- args[[2]]
out_path <- args[[3]]

library(rray4, lib.loc = lib)

set.seed(1)

dbl <- function(...) array(runif(prod(c(...))), c(...))
int <- function(...) array(sample(100L, prod(c(...)), TRUE), c(...))

m <- dbl(4000, 4000)
cube <- dbl(200, 200, 200)
wide <- dbl(10, 1e6)
narrow <- dbl(2, 5e6)
deep <- dbl(rep(2, 20))

mi <- int(4000, 4000)
wide_i <- int(10, 1e6)
narrow_i <- int(2, 5e6)

cases <- list(
  "add dbl [4000, 4000] + [4000, 4000]" = local({
    y <- dbl(4000, 4000)
    \() rray_add(m, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [4000, 4000] + [4000, 1]" = local({
    y <- dbl(4000, 1)
    \() rray_add(m, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [4000, 4000] + [1, 4000]" = local({
    y <- dbl(1, 4000)
    \() rray_add(m, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [4000, 4000] + [1, 1]" = local({
    y <- dbl(1, 1)
    \() rray_add(m, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [4000, 1] + [1, 4000]" = local({
    x <- dbl(4000, 1)
    y <- dbl(1, 4000)
    \() rray_add(x, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [200, 200, 200] + [1, 200, 1]" = local({
    y <- dbl(1, 200, 1)
    \() rray_add(cube, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [10, 1e6] + [10, 1]" = local({
    y <- dbl(10, 1)
    \() rray_add(wide, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [10, 1e6] + [1, 1e6]" = local({
    y <- dbl(1, 1e6)
    \() rray_add(wide, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [2, 5e6] + [2, 1]" = local({
    y <- dbl(2, 1)
    \() rray_add(narrow, y)
  }),
  "add dbl [2] x 20 + [2, 1] x 10" = local({
    y <- dbl(rep(c(2, 1), 10))
    \() rray_add(deep, y)
  }),
  "add int [4000, 4000] + [4000, 4000]" = local({
    y <- int(4000, 4000)
    \() rray_add(mi, y)
  }),
  "add int [4000, 4000] + [4000, 1]" = local({
    y <- int(4000, 1)
    \() rray_add(mi, y)
  }),
  "add int [4000, 4000] + [1, 4000]" = local({
    y <- int(1, 4000)
    \() rray_add(mi, y)
  }),
  "add int [10, 1e6] + [10, 1]" = local({
    y <- int(10, 1)
    \() rray_add(wide_i, y)
  }),
  "add int [10, 1e6] + [1, 1e6]" = local({
    y <- int(1, 1e6)
    \() rray_add(wide_i, y)
  }),
  "add int [2, 5e6] + [2, 1]" = local({
    y <- int(2, 1)
    \() rray_add(narrow_i, y)
  }),
  "add int + dbl [4000, 4000] + [4000, 4000]" = local({
    \() rray_add(mi, m)
  }),
  "sum dbl [4000, 4000] over 1" = \() rray_sum(m, 1L),
  "sum dbl [4000, 4000] over 2" = \() rray_sum(m, 2L),
  "sum dbl [4000, 4000] over 1, 2" = \() rray_sum(m, 1:2),
  "sum dbl [200, 200, 200] over 1" = \() rray_sum(cube, 1L),
  "sum dbl [200, 200, 200] over 2" = \() rray_sum(cube, 2L),
  "sum dbl [200, 200, 200] over 3" = \() rray_sum(cube, 3L),
  "sum dbl [200, 200, 200] over 1, 3" = \() rray_sum(cube, c(1L, 3L)),
  "sum dbl [10, 1e6] over 1" = \() rray_sum(wide, 1L),
  "sum dbl [10, 1e6] over 2" = \() rray_sum(wide, 2L),
  "sum dbl [2, 5e6] over 1" = \() rray_sum(narrow, 1L),
  "sum dbl [2, 5e6] over 2" = \() rray_sum(narrow, 2L),
  "sum dbl [2] x 20 over odd axes" = \() rray_sum(deep, seq(1L, 19L, 2L)),
  "sum dbl na_rm [4000, 4000] over 1" = \() rray_sum(m, 1L, na_rm = TRUE),
  "sum int [4000, 4000] over 1" = \() rray_sum(mi, 1L),
  "sum int [4000, 4000] over 2" = \() rray_sum(mi, 2L),
  "sum int [10, 1e6] over 1" = \() rray_sum(wide_i, 1L),
  "sum int [2, 5e6] over 1" = \() rray_sum(narrow_i, 1L),
  "tiny add dbl [2, 3] + [2, 1]" = local({
    x <- dbl(2, 3)
    y <- dbl(2, 1)
    \() rray_add(x, y)
  }),
  "tiny sum dbl [2, 3] over 1" = local({
    x <- dbl(2, 3)
    \() rray_sum(x, 1L)
  })
)

if (length(args) >= 4 && nzchar(args[[4]])) {
  cases <- cases[grepl(args[[4]], names(cases))]
}

hashes <- vapply(cases, \(f) rlang::hash(f()), character(1))

times <- vapply(
  names(cases),
  function(name) {
    f <- cases[[name]]
    tiny <- startsWith(name, "tiny")
    gc()
    result <- bench::mark(
      f(),
      iterations = if (tiny) 20000 else 25,
      check = FALSE,
      filter_gc = FALSE,
      memory = FALSE
    )
    as.numeric(result$median) * if (tiny) 1e6 else 1e3
  },
  numeric(1)
)

saveRDS(list(label = label, times = times, hashes = hashes), out_path)

Inside a run the loops are much the same in both builds. The old RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_NEXT() reads the plan through a pointer, while the new next() reads it from the local struct, so the difference is most likely in the step between runs. I'll wait for the full numbers before drawing conclusions.

Bash: git worktree remove --force /private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/f7982…

Add and sum now use the merged run iterator. Add is unaffected, and sum is either the same or faster: up to 34% faster when runs are short. All 3497 tests pass. The change is uncommitted, and I've removed the baseline worktree.

Benchmark setup: the last commit (6cc1e10, add and sum still on the old macros) against this working tree. 15 rounds × 36 cases, with the order rotated each round. Outputs were identical in all 30 runs.

Add (17 cases)

  • Every case is within ±3.5% of the old code, inside the 4% to 27% spread between rounds. That covers double, integer and integer + double, all four stride cases, short runs of 2 and 10, and the 20-axis case.

  • The short-run case that was 4% to 6% slower in the plan's earlier measurements, [10, 1e6] + [10, 1], is now 2% faster.

Sum (19 cases)

  • Long runs: the same (±1.5%).

  • Short runs: clearly faster.

    Case Old New
    double [10, 1e6] over 1 3.84 ms 3.22 ms (−16%)
    double [2, 5e6] over 1 15.65 ms 12.39 ms (−21%)
    double [2] × 20 over odd axes 2.01 ms 1.32 ms (−34%)
    integer [2, 5e6] over 1 15.10 ms 13.78 ms (−9%)

    The loops inside each run are the same as before, so the gain must come from stepping between runs. The old RRAY_STRIDED_ITERATOR_NEXT() reads the dimensions and strides through the plan pointer, while the new next() reads them from the local struct. I haven't pinned down exactly which instructions account for it.

  • Tiny [2, 3] over 1: 0.90 µs before, 0.94 µs after (+4.5%). That's about 40 ns per call, and the spread is 9%, so it might be noise. If it's real, the likely cause is building the larger struct on every call.

The one regression still standing is the raw broadcast from before: 9% to 13% slower on very short runs. Add and sum don't show it, because each element does more work.

How it's wired

  • rray_binary_arithmetic() and rray_reduce() are shared with other operations and pass the old plan. So I added rray_binary_arithmetic_run() (in src/arithmetic.c) and rray_reduce_run() (in src/reduce.c) next to them. They work out the broadcast strides and pass the raw shape to the kernel. Add packs the x and y strides into one array, ordered axis by axis. Once every operation has moved over, the old drivers can be deleted and the _run versions renamed.

  • The loops live in macros local to each file, RRAY_ADD() in src/arithmetic-add.c and RRAY_SUM() in src/reduce-sum.c, and each is #undef'd at the end of its file. The loop bodies are copied exactly from RRAY_BINARY() and RRAY_REDUCE(), so the numbers measure only the iterator. Add still includes binary.h, but only for RRAY_BINARY_ARGS() and RRAY_BINARY_NO_ARGS.

  • I haven't added the plan's local running total for sum when the output stride is 0. It's a separate change to the loop body, so it should be measured on its own.

Do you want me to commit this, try the local running total next, or both?

Davis

I’ve realized in RRAY ADD the 4 branches should be

if (x==0 & y==0)
Else if (x==0)
Else if (y==0)
Else

Also, since you basically just created RRAY BINARY, just include it as RRAY BINARY RUN in the header?

+#define RRAY_BINARY_RUN(                                                       \
+  X_CTYPE,                                                                     \
+  X_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
+  X_CAST,                                                                      \
+  Y_CTYPE,                                                                     \
+  Y_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
+  Y_CAST,                                                                      \
+  OUT_RTYPE,                                                                   \
+  OUT_CTYPE,                                                                   \
+  OUT_DEREF,                                                                   \
+  ONE,                                                                         \
+  ONE_ARGS                                                                     \
+)                                                                              \
+  struct rray_run_iterator it =                                                \
+    rray_run_iterator(v_dimensions, dimensionality, v_strides, 2);             \
+                                                                               \
+  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(OUT_RTYPE, rray_run_iterator_size(&it)));   \
+  OUT_CTYPE* v_out = OUT_DEREF(out);                                           \
+                                                                               \
+  const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);                                       \
+  const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y);                                       \
+                                                                               \
+  for (; !rray_run_iterator_done(&it); rray_run_iterator_next(&it, 2)) {       \
+    const r_ssize start = rray_run_iterator_start(&it);                        \
+    const r_ssize end = rray_run_iterator_end(&it);                            \
+                                                                               \
+    r_ssize x_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(&it, 0);                             \
+    const r_ssize x_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(&it, 0);                 \
+                                                                               \
+    r_ssize y_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(&it, 1);                             \
+    const r_ssize y_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(&it, 1);                 \
+                                                                               \
+    if (x_stride == 0 && y_stride == 0) {                                      \
+      const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                        \
+      const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                        \
+      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
+        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
+      }                                                                        \
+    } else if (x_stride == 0) {                                                \
+      const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                        \
+      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
+        const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                      \
+        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
+        y_loc += y_stride;                                                     \
+      }                                                                        \
+    } else if (y_stride == 0) {                                                \
+      const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                        \
+      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
+        const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                      \
+        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
+        x_loc += x_stride;                                                     \
+      }                                                                        \
+    } else {                                                                   \
+      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
+        const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                      \
+        const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                      \
+        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
+        x_loc += x_stride;                                                     \
+        y_loc += y_stride;                                                     \
+      }                                                                        \
+    }                                                                          \
+  }                                                                            \
+                                                                               \
+  FREE(1);                                                                     \
+  return out;
+
+#endif

32	static rray_reduce_run_fn rray_sum_switch(
33	  r_obj* x,
34	  bool na_rm,
35	  struct rray_arg* arg,
36	  struct r_lazy error_call
37	) {
38	  switch (rray_typeof(x)) {
39	  case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
40	    return na_rm ? rray_sum_lgl_na_rm : rray_sum_lgl;
41	  case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
42	    return na_rm ? rray_sum_int_na_rm : rray_sum_int;
43	  case RRAY_TYPE_double:
44	    return na_rm ? rray_sum_dbl_na_rm : rray_sum_dbl;
45	  case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
46	    return na_rm ? rray_sum_cpl_na_rm : rray_sum_cpl;
47	
48	  case RRAY_TYPE_character:
49	  case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
50	  case RRAY_TYPE_list:
51	    stop_unsupported_reduce("sum", x, arg, error_call);
52	
53	  case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
54	    stop_scalar_input(x, arg, error_call);
55	  }
56	
57	  r_stop_unreachable();
58	}

 9 files changed, 480 insertions(+), 78 deletions(-)

Both changes are done. All 3497 tests pass, and nothing is committed.

RRAY_BINARY_RUN() (in src/binary.h)

  • The four branches are now flat: both strides 0, then x's stride 0, then y's stride 0, then neither.

  • In the first branch, ONE() is still called once per element, as it was before. Calling it once before the loop and filling the run with the result (the way the plan's add example does) would be a small, separate change.

  • RRAY_ADD() is gone. The 16 add kernels call RRAY_BINARY_RUN(), and src/arithmetic-add.c no longer includes strided-iterator2.h directly, because binary.h includes it now.

  • Speed against the nested branches (8 rounds): 16 of 18 add cases are within ±3%. The two outliers are integer [2, 5e6] + [2, 1] (+9%) and the double 20-axis case (−9%). They point in opposite directions and only 8 rounds back them, so I read them as noise. I can run more rounds if you want to be sure.

RRAY_REDUCE_RUN() (in src/reduce.h)

  • It's the same macro as RRAY_SUM(), moved to sit right after RRAY_REDUCE(). reduce.h now includes strided-iterator2.h.

  • The 8 sum kernels call it, and RRAY_SUM() and its #undef are gone. Nothing in the macro itself changed, so I didn't benchmark it again.
